  22. ## **Section 1: Introduction**

      1. ### **1.1 Welcome to Soulvegr RPG**

Soulvegr RPG is a tabletop roleplaying game set in an infinite multiverse where ancient myth collides with cutting-edge technology—and everything in between. The cosmos of the Soulvegr is boundless: medieval kingdoms where knights battle dragons exist alongside neon-drenched megacities where corporations trade in immortality. On one world, an Elven wizard tends a sacred grove on a planet that has never seen a starship. On another, a cybernetic operative downloads stolen memories into a cloned body while dodging corporate kill-squads. Both worlds are real. Both are connected by the **Soulvegr**—the vast, luminous network through which all souls travel between life, death, and rebirth.

You are an **Initiate**—a hero of exceptional potential standing at the threshold of greatness. You might hail from a primitive world where magic is whispered folklore, or from a towering Spire city where gods walk openly and death is merely an expensive inconvenience. Regardless of your origin, the multiverse is about to crack open beneath your feet.

Every being in the Soulvegr is defined by three fundamental aspects:

* **Vessel**: The physical body they inhabit—flesh, synth-frame, or something stranger.
* **Essence**: The mind, memories, and personality that make them who they are.
* **Soul**: The eternal spark that connects them to the astral plane and the deeper forces of existence.

For most mortals on most worlds, these three aspects are inseparable—fused from birth until death. You are born. You live. You die.

But in the advanced corners of the multiverse, technology and forbidden knowledge have learned to manipulate these boundaries. **Soul Link Devices (SLDs)** can tether a soul to a new body through technological means, mimicking what divine magic accomplishes through astral cords. Vessels can be swapped like suits of armor. Essence can be backed up, edited, or stolen. Immortality is real—but it is expensive, dangerous, and controlled by powers who would rather see you dead than share their secrets.

The Soulvegr is not a safe place. It is a crucible where souls are tested and tempered, where empires rise and fall across a million worlds, and where the **12 Divine Houses** wage endless wars for supremacy. And far above them all, in realms beyond mortal comprehension, the **Eternals** watch with silent, inscrutable intent.

2. ### **1.2 Your Story, Your Way**

Soulvegr RPG rejects rigid classes in favor of total creative freedom. Using a **150 Build Point (BP)** system, you construct your hero from the ground up by defining their Vessel, Soul, and Essence.

**Classless Freedom**: There are no "Fighters" or "Wizards" here. You might be a psychic starship captain, a dwarven mech-engineer who worships a forge-god, an orcish void-mercenary with a stolen SLD, or a medieval knight who has never heard of plasma rifles. The tools are in your hands.

**A Unified Cosmos**: Whether you're casting spells on a fantasy world or hacking a corporate mainframe in a Spire, you're tapping into the same six fundamental forces—the **Six Core Attributes**: Authority, Force, Vitality, Mind, Substance, and Spirit. Magic and technology are merely different expressions of the same underlying reality.

**The Three Epochs**: You begin in the **Initiate Epoch** (Levels 1–10) as a grounded but extraordinary hero. As you earn **Advancement Points (AP)**, you evolve into a living legend in the **Advanced Epoch** (Levels 11–20), and eventually ascend to demigod status in the **Ascended Epoch** (Levels 21–30), capable of reshaping reality itself.

**The Weight of Choice**: Every action ripples outward. You'll manage Credits, equipment, and soul-touched resources to survive and thrive. Your reputation and deeds determine whether the 12 Houses see you as a pawn, a threat, or a legend worth recruiting—or eliminating.

3. ### **1.3 This Handbook: Your Guide**

This Player's Handbook equips you to create and play an Initiate. It's designed to be accessible for newcomers while providing tactical depth for veterans.

**Core Mechanics (Section 3)**: The essential rules of play. Learn how to resolve actions using the Six Core Attributes, manage Health and Energy, and understand the stakes of combat and death.

**Character Creation (Section 6)**: Your step-by-step guide to spending 150 Build Points on Vessel, Soul, and Essence—defining your race, Attributes, Skills, Talents, and starting gear.

**The Powers System (Section 13)**: Unified rules for all supernatural, technological, and martial abilities available to Initiates.

**The Universe (Section 14)**: A primer on the infinite multiverse, the Soulvegr itself, the 12 Houses vying for control, and the distant Eternals who watch from beyond.

**New players**: Master Sections 3–5 first. GMs can run compelling sessions with just the d20 mechanics and resource management.

The Soulvegr stretches before you. A million worlds. A million ways to die. One soul.

**Walk the path.**

23. ## **Section 2: Getting Started**

    1. ### **2.1 What You Need to Play**

Before embarking on your cosmic journey through the Soulvegr, gather these essentials:

**Players**: One player serves as the **Game Master (GM)**, who narrates the universe, controls allies and adversaries, and adjudicates the rules. The ideal group includes 3–6 additional players, each controlling one Initiate hero standing at the threshold of legend.

**Dice**: You'll need a **d20** for all core action resolution, plus a full set of polyhedral dice (d4, d6, d8, d10, d12) for damage, effects, and special abilities.

**Character Sheets**: These track your Six Core Attributes, Health and Energy pools, Build Points (BP), Advancement Points (AP), and the three pillars of your character—Vessel, Soul, and Essence. Sheets also record gear such as weapons, armor, and Soul Link Devices (SLDs).

**Imagination and Cooperation**: The true fuel of Soulvegr RPG is collaborative storytelling. When disputes arise over rules or outcomes, resolve them in a way that serves the story and keeps the game moving. The narrative always comes first.

2. ### **2.2 How to Use This Handbook**

This handbook serves as both tutorial for newcomers and reference guide for veterans.

**New Players**: Begin with **Section 3 (Core Mechanics)** to master the fundamental rules—d20 checks, Attributes, and resource pools. Then proceed to **Section 6 (Character Creation)** to build your hero step by step.

**Game Masters**: Focus on **Core Mechanics (Section 3)**, **Character Advancement (Section 5)**, and **The Powers System (Section 13)** to run sessions smoothly and understand character progression.

**Experienced Players**: Use the **Table of Contents** to jump directly to specific rules, races, Powers, or setting lore as needed.

**The Golden Rule**: If any rule hinders fun, drama, or the flow of your story, the GM has full authority to adapt or ignore it. Soulvegr RPG is a framework for epic tales—not a straitjacket. When in doubt, prioritize enjoyment and narrative momentum over rigid adherence to mechanics.

3. ### **2.3 Key Terms \& Definitions**

Familiarize yourself with the core vocabulary of the Soulvegr to navigate its infinite expanse.

1. #### **Resource Economy**

**Build Points (BP)**: The currency of character creation. You begin with 150 BP to purchase your Core Attributes, race, Skills, Talents, Perks, starting Powers, and initial gear.

**Advancement Points (AP)**: Earned through gameplay and awarded at the end of each session. Spend AP to increase Attributes, purchase new Powers, improve Skills, or unlock advanced abilities as your hero evolves.

**Credits (Cr)**: The universal medium of exchange across civilized space. Use Credits to purchase weapons, armor, Frames, Soul Link Devices, transportation, services, and black-market goods.

2. #### **The Character Engine**

**The Three Pillars**: Every character is defined by three interconnected aspects:

* **Vessel**: Your physical body—flesh, synth-frame, war-chassis, or hybrid.
* **Soul**: Your eternal spark, residing in the astral plane and connected to your Vessel via natural cord or technological Soul Link Device.
* **Essence**: Your mind, memories, personality, skills, and identity—the "you" that persists across Vessels.

**The Six Core Attributes**: The fundamental forces that define your capabilities—**Authority, Force, Vitality, Mind, Substance, and Spirit**. These govern all actions, from swinging a sword to hacking a neural network to channeling divine fire.

**Epoch**: Your power tier. This handbook covers the **Initiate Epoch (Levels 1–10)**, representing grounded but exceptional heroes. Higher Epochs unlock legendary and godlike abilities.

**Defense Value (DV)**: The target number opponents must meet or exceed on their attack rolls to hit you. Calculated from your Attributes, gear, and abilities.

**Health Points (HP)**: The physical durability of your current Vessel. When HP reaches 0, your Vessel is destroyed or dying—potentially severing the connection to your Soul.

**Energy Points (EP)**: Metaphysical fuel generated by your Soul. Spend EP to activate Powers, execute advanced maneuvers, and fuel technological or supernatural abilities.

**Soul Link Device (SLD)**: A technological implant that tethers your Soul (residing in the astral plane) to your current Vessel. If your SLD is destroyed while you lack a backup connection, you face **true death**—your Soul severed and lost.

**Frame / Vessel**: Interchangeable terms for the body you currently inhabit. **Frames** typically refer to synthetic, cybernetic, or heavily augmented bodies. **Vessels** usually denote organic, cloned, or divinely crafted forms. Both serve the same function: housing your Essence and anchoring your Soul.

3. #### **The Universe**

**The Infinite Multiverse**: A boundless tapestry of realities—primitive fantasy worlds where dragons rule the skies, gleaming Spire cities where gods broker power in boardrooms, war-torn frontiers consumed by the Void, and everything between. All are connected by the Soulvegr.

**The Soulvegr**: The vast, luminous network through which all souls travel—a cosmic highway connecting life, death, and rebirth across the multiverse. Souls journey through the Soulvegr aboard **Tulip Freighters**, guided by psychopomps and divine servants toward their ultimate fate.

**The 12 Divine Houses**: Ambitious pantheons of gods, demigods, and their mortal champions who wage endless crusades for dominance across the multiverse. Each House commands vast armies, controls vital resources, and interprets the cosmic order to suit their agenda.

**The Eternals**: Twelve silent, unknowable entities who exist in realms beyond mortal comprehension. The Houses claim to serve them, but the Eternals remain distant and inscrutable—watching, waiting, judging by laws mortals cannot fathom.

**The Void**: The hungry, chaotic anti-reality that lurks between worlds. It births horrors, consumes civilizations, and warps those who venture too close into Aberrants—twisted mockeries of their former selves.

**The Eternal Throne**: A legendary artifact or seat of power whispered to grant dominion over the fundamental laws of existence itself. Whether myth or terrible truth, entire civilizations have been destroyed in its pursuit.

**Here's the revised Section 3 with enhanced clarity and professional polish:**

24. ## **Section 3: Core Mechanics**

    1. ### **3.1 The d20 Check: Resolving Actions**

Whenever your Initiate attempts an action with uncertain outcome—swinging a blade, hacking a terminal, persuading a House agent, or resisting a Void-touched anomaly—you make a **Check**. This is the fundamental resolution mechanic of Soulvegr RPG.

1. #### **The Formula**

**Total = d20 + Attribute Modifier + Skill Rank + Other Bonuses**

1. **Roll a d20** and add your relevant Attribute modifier and Skill Rank.
2. **Compare the total** against a **Difficulty Class (DC)** set by the GM, or an opponent's **Defense Value (DV)**.

   #### **Interpreting Results**

   **Success**: If your total meets or exceeds the DC or DV, you succeed at your intended action.

   **Failure**: If your total falls short, you fail to achieve your goal—though failure often creates new opportunities or complications rather than simply halting the story.

   **Critical Success (Natural 20)**: Rolling a natural 20 on the d20 is an automatic success, often resulting in maximum damage, a significant tactical advantage, or a dramatic narrative flourish that exceeds your original intent.

   **Critical Failure (Natural 1)**: Rolling a natural 1 on the d20 is an automatic failure, typically resulting in a complication, tactical setback, or narrative consequence that worsens your situation.

2. ### **3.2 Health \& Energy: The Two Resource Pools**

   In the Soulvegr, your body is equipment and your Soul is eternal. You manage two distinct pools representing their endurance.

1. #### **Health Points (HP)**

   HP represents the physical durability of your current Vessel or Frame—how much damage it can withstand before catastrophic failure.

   **Reaching Zero**: When your HP drops to 0, your Vessel is destroyed or dying, immediately triggering the **Death Sequence** (detailed in Section 4.3). Whether you survive depends on the integrity of your Soul Link Device, available backup connections, and how quickly allies can stabilize or reconnect you.

   **Formula**: **HP = 10 + Vitality Score + Force Score**

   *Example: An Initiate with Vitality 14 and Force 12 has 36 HP (10 + 14 + 12).*

2. #### **Energy Points (EP)**

   EP represents your Soul's internal reservoir—the metaphysical fuel your Soul Link Device channels into action. It powers magical spells, advanced technological abilities, psionic techniques, and superhuman feats.

   **Spending EP**: Activating Powers and special abilities costs EP. Once depleted, you cannot use these abilities until you rest or otherwise recover EP.

   **Frame Burnout**: Every Vessel has a **Burnout Threshold**—a limit on how much EP you can safely channel per scene without damaging the Frame itself. Exceed this threshold, and your Vessel begins taking direct HP damage as it physically overloads from channeling too much metaphysical energy. Cheap street-clinic Frames burn out quickly; military-grade or divine Vessels can safely channel immense power.

   **Formula**: **EP = 10 + Mind Score + Spirit Score**

   *Example: An Initiate with Mind 13 and Spirit 15 has 38 EP (10 + 13 + 15).*

3. ### **3.3 The Six Core Attributes**

   Every being in the Soulvegr is defined by their connection to the **Six Core Attributes**—the fundamental forces through which a Soul expresses itself across physical, mental, and metaphysical reality. These Attributes govern every action in the cosmos, from swinging a sword to hacking a corporate mainframe to channeling divine fire.

|Attribute|Governs|Common Uses|
|-|-|-|
|**Authority**|**Leadership, charisma, social influence**|**Persuasion, intimidation, command, diplomacy**|
|**Force**|**Raw physical power and kinetic energy**|**Melee attacks, lifting, breaking objects, wrestling**|
|**Vitality**|**Endurance, resilience, biological stability**|**Resisting poison/disease, HP calculation, endurance**|
|**Mind**|**Logic, memory, precision, technical mastery**|**Hacking, engineering, ranged attacks, investigation**|
|**Substance**|**Matter manipulation, crafting, physical form**|**Forging gear, repairs, augmentation, creation**|
|**Spirit**|**Willpower, intuition, soul-strength**|**Magic/Powers, resisting fear/mental attacks, EP calculation**|

1. #### **Attribute Modifiers**

Your Attribute **score** (typically ranging from 8–18 for Initiates) determines your **modifier**, which you add to relevant d20 checks.

**Formula**: **Modifier = (Attribute Score - 10) ÷ 2 (rounded down)**

*Example: An Attribute score of 14 grants a +2 modifier. A score of 9 grants a -1 modifier.*

4. ### **3.4 The Nature of Conflict**

Conflict in the Soulvegr is cinematic, visceral, and potentially lethal. While the core d20 resolution mechanic remains constant throughout your journey, the **scale and stakes** of conflict evolve dramatically as you advance through the Epochs.

1. #### **Initiate Epoch (Levels 1–10): Street-Level Survival**

This is your beginning. You are exceptional but mortal—a cyberpunk street operative, a medieval knight facing their first dragon, or a starship gunner in a desperate fleet engagement. Your weapons might be plasma rifles, runeblades, or neural hacking interfaces. Combat is fast, brutal, and deadly. A well-placed shot or blade can end you. Resources matter. Every Credit, every piece of gear, every ally counts.

2. #### **Advanced Epoch (Levels 11–20): Legendary Heroes**

You have transcended mortality. You wield experimental god-tech, command elite forces, or channel Powers that reshape battlefields. You might lead House strike teams, pilot reality-warping mechs, or walk through small-arms fire unharmed. The stakes escalate—planetary conflicts, House politics, cosmic artifacts.

3. #### **Ascended Epoch (Levels 21–30): Demigods and Cosmic Warlords**

You stand among the divine. Reality bends to your will. You command fleets that span star systems, wield Powers that rewrite the laws of physics, and negotiate directly with the 12 Houses—or challenge them. Your conflicts determine the fate of entire civilizations. The tools (advanced SLDs, divine Frames, reality anchors) and consequences (True Death, multiverse collapse, Eternal attention) become profound.

**The underlying mechanics remain the same—but the scope of what you can achieve becomes limitless.**

25. ## **Section 4: How to Play the Game**

This section covers the core gameplay loops of Soulvegr RPG—movement, combat, exploration, and social encounters across the infinite multiverse.

1. ### **4.1 Time, Movement \& Speed**

Soulvegr RPG uses three time scales to match any pace of play, from intense firefights to interstellar voyages.

3. # 

|Scale|Duration|Used For|
|-|-|-|
|**Round**|**6 seconds**|**Combat turns and time-sensitive actions**|
|**Scene**|**Minutes to hours**|**Exploration, conversations, investigations, puzzles**|
|**Downtime**|**Days to years**|**Long-distance travel, crafting projects, training**|

1. #### **Base Speed**

Your Speed determines how far you can move with a single Move action (measured in feet per round).

|Species/Size|Base Speed|
|-|-|
|**Humans, most Medium creatures**|**30 ft**|
|**Dwarves, Gnomes, Halflings**|**25 ft**|
|**Elves, Orcs, Goliaths**|**35 ft**|

**Frame and Vessel Modifications: Your choice of body may alter your Speed. A heavy War-Chassis might reduce Speed by 5 ft but grant substantial armor bonuses, while a lightweight Synth-Frame could add 5 ft at the cost of durability.**

2. #### **Racial Movement Traits (Examples)**
* **Dwarf**: Immune to Speed penalties from difficult terrain; may Dash as a Bonus Action.
* **Elf**: Advantage on checks to avoid being knocked Prone or to maintain balance.
* **Spacer**: +10 ft Speed in zero-gravity environments.

  #### **Movement Mechanics**

  **Difficult Terrain**: Moving through rubble, thick vegetation, shallow water, or similar obstacles costs 2 feet of movement for every 1 foot traveled.

  **Swimming and Climbing**: Costs 2 feet of movement per 1 foot traveled unless you have a climbing or swimming Speed.

  **Jumping**:

* **Long Jump**: Distance equal to your Force score in feet (requires 10-foot running start; half distance from standing position).
* **High Jump**: Height equal to 3 + your Force modifier in feet (requires 10-foot running start).

  **Falling**: Take 1d6 bludgeoning damage per 10 feet fallen (maximum 20d6). You land Prone unless you have an ability that prevents it.

2. ### **4.2 Actions in Combat**

   On your turn, you can take one **Move** (up to your Speed) and one **Action**. You may also take a **Bonus Action** and/or **Reactions** if granted by Powers, gear, or special circumstances.

1. #### **Standard Actions**

|Action|Effect|
|-|-|
|**Attack**|**Make one melee or ranged attack roll against a target's DV.**|
|**Activate Power**|**Use a spell, technological ability, or maneuver (typically costs EP).**|
|**Dash**|**Move up to your Speed again this turn.**|
|**Dodge**|**All attacks against you have Disadvantage until the start of your next turn.**|
|**Help**|**Grant an ally Advantage on their next ability check or attack roll against a target within 5 feet of you.**|
|**Hide**|**Make a contested check (typically Force or Mind for Stealth) against observers' passive Perception. Requires cover or concealment.**|
|**Brace**|**Add your Vitality modifier to your DV until the start of your next turn as you take a defensive stance.**|

2. #### **Combat Maneuvers**

These special attacks replace one Attack action. When contested, ties go to the defender.

**Grapple**: Make a contested Force (Athletics) check against the target. On success, the target's Speed becomes 0 until the grapple ends.

**Shove**: Make a contested Force (Athletics) check against the target. On success, knock the target Prone or push them 5 feet away from you.

3. #### **Reactions**

Reactions occur outside your turn in response to specific triggers. You get one Reaction per round.

**Opportunity Attack**: When a creature you can see moves out of your melee reach without using the Disengage action, you may make one melee attack against them.

**Readied Action**: On your turn, you may declare a trigger condition and an action. When the trigger occurs before your next turn, you may use your Reaction to perform that action.

3. ### **4.3 The Combat Sequence**

Combat unfolds in a structured sequence to maintain tactical clarity and dramatic tension.

1. #### **1. Surprise**

The GM determines if any combatants are unaware of the threat. Surprised characters cannot act during the first round of combat and cannot take Reactions until their first turn ends.

2. #### **2. Establish the Battlefield**

The GM describes the environment—terrain features, cover, hazards, lighting conditions, and positions of all combatants.

3. #### **3. Roll Initiative**

Each combatant rolls **d20 + Initiative modifier**.

You may use either:

* **Force modifier** (for reflexes and physical reaction speed), or
* **Mind modifier** (for tactical awareness and threat assessment)

Choose which modifier to use before rolling. The highest total acts first. Ties are resolved by comparing the relevant Attribute scores (Force or Mind); if still tied, roll d20 to break the tie.

4. #### **4. Take Turns**

Combatants act in Initiative order, taking their Move, Action, and any Bonus Actions or Reactions. When everyone has acted, the round ends and a new round begins. Continue until the conflict is resolved.

4. ### **The Death Sequence: What Happens at 0 HP**

When your HP reaches 0, your Vessel or Frame is destroyed or critically failing. What happens next depends on whether you possess a Soul Link Device.

1. #### **With a Soul Link Device (SLD)**

Your Soul remains tethered via your SLD. Make a **Survival Check**:

**Roll d20 + Vitality modifier vs. your SLD's Survival DC** (typically 12–18 depending on SLD quality).

**Success**: Your SLD enters dormant mode, preserving your Soul-to-Vessel connection. You are unconscious but stable, awaiting medical aid or Relinking to a new Vessel.

**Failure**: Your SLD is destroyed in the catastrophic damage. Your Soul is severed from your Vessel. This is **True Death**—your consciousness floats in the astral plane, vulnerable to predators, dissolution, or the journey to Soulvegr.

2. #### **Without a Soul Link Device**

If you rely on a natural astral cord (as most primitive-world beings do), you fall unconscious at 0 HP and must make **Death Saving Throws**.

Each round you remain at 0 HP, roll **d20 + Spirit modifier vs. DC 15**:

* **Three successes**: You stabilize at 1 HP.
* **Three failures**: You die. Your Soul is severed and begins its journey through the astral plane.

**Additional damage** taken while at 0 HP counts as **automatic failure(s)** (1 failure for normal damage; 2 failures for a critical hit).

3. #### **Targeted SLD Destruction**

An enemy who knows you possess an SLD can attempt to destroy it directly with a **called shot**. This attack is made at **Disadvantage** but, if successful, results in **immediate True Death** regardless of your remaining HP. The SLD is shattered, your Soul is severed, and you enter the astral plane.

5. ### **Cover and Concealment**

Cover provides protection from attacks and area effects.

|Cover Type|Benefit|
|-|-|
|**Half Cover**|**+2 bonus to DV and saving throws**|
|**Three-Quarters Cover**|**+5 bonus to DV and saving throws**|
|**Warded Cover**|**+2 (or +5) to DV/saves, plus add your Mind or Spirit modifier (magical or technological shielding)**|

6. ### **4.4 Exploration and Discovery**

   1. #### **Travel Pace**

When traveling overland or through hostile territory, choose your pace:

|Pace|Effect|
|-|-|
|**Cautious**|**Stealth is possible; party cannot be surprised; very slow progress**|
|**Normal**|**Standard speed; balanced awareness**|
|**Fast**|**Cover ground quickly; -5 penalty to all Perception checks**|

2. #### **Rest and Recovery**

|Rest Type|Duration|Recovery|
|-|-|-|
|**Short Rest**|**1 hour**|**Recover half your maximum EP (rounded down). Spend Hit Dice (roll d8 per level + Vitality modifier) to regain HP.**|
|**Long Rest**|**8 hours**|**Fully restore HP and EP. Regain half your spent Hit Dice (minimum 1). Can only benefit from one Long Rest per 24-hour period.**|
|**Sanctuary Rest**|**8 hours in sacred, shielded, or divinely protected space**|**Gain Long Rest benefits plus remove one level of Exhaustion or one minor Echo Scar (lingering Soul/Essence trauma).**|

**Note: Void-corrupted zones, Breach-infested areas, or sites of cosmic disturbance may block rest entirely or reduce its effectiveness. The GM determines whether rest is possible in such hostile environments.**

7. ### **4.5 The Social Battlefield**

Not all conflicts are solved with violence. Social encounters utilize **Authority** (leadership and persuasion), **Spirit** (insight and intuition), and **Mind** (analysis and deception) to navigate diplomacy, intrigue, and manipulation.

1. #### **Influencing NPCs**

The GM assigns a base DC based on the target's current attitude toward you:

|Target Attitude|Base DC|
|-|-|
|**Friendly**|**10**|
|**Indifferent**|**15**|
|**Hostile**|**20+**|

**Modifiers: The GM may adjust the DC based on context—offering bribes, appealing to shared interests, leveraging blackmail, or making threats.**

2. #### **Leverage Tokens**

Exceptional roleplaying or a **Critical Success** on a social check earns a **Leverage Token**—a narrative currency the player can spend later to:

* Gain Advantage on a related social check
* Call in a favor from that NPC
* Introduce a helpful coincidence tied to that relationship

  2. ## **Section 5: Character Advancement**

In Soulvegr RPG, characters do not advance through body counts or treasure hoards alone. They evolve through narrative milestones, collaborative storytelling, and the gradual transcendence of mortal limitations. While the system remains classless, your hero progresses through **Levels 1–30**, earning **Advancement Points (AP)** to enhance Attributes, acquire Skills, and unlock reality-bending Powers. True advancement—the leap from one **Epoch** to the next—requires more than accumulated points. It demands guidance, heroic catalysts, and the discovery of your cosmic potential.

1. ### **5.1 Session Scoring \& Advancement Points**

Rather than tracking individual kills or loot, the entire group advances together based on the quality of each session. At the end of every session, the Game Master scores the group across five categories on a scale of **0 (Needs Improvement)**, **1 (Good)**, or **2 (Exceptional)**.

|Category|Focus|Criteria for Points|
|-|-|-|
|**Readiness**|**Punctuality \& Preparation**|**Was everyone on time? Were character sheets, dice, and materials ready? Did turns flow smoothly?**|
|**Accomplishment**|**Objectives \& Progress**|**Did the party achieve goals, survive encounters, or meaningfully advance the plot?**|
|**Teamwork**|**Group Cohesion**|**Did players support each other tactically and narratively? Were conflicts handled in-character?**|
|**Immersion**|**Engagement \& Investment**|**Was there tension, excitement, or genuine emotional investment in the unfolding story?**|
|**Roleplay**|**Character Acting**|**Did players make decisions rooted in their character's personality, history, and motivations?**|

1. #### **The Leveling Loop**

**Maximum Score per Session**: 10 Points

**Threshold to Level Up**: 20 Points

When the group accumulates **20 Points**, everyone gains a **Level**. Excess points carry over to the next level.

*Example: If a group has 18 accumulated points and earns 10 in their next session, they gain a level and begin progress toward the next with 8 points already banked.*

2. ### **5.2 The Three Epochs of the Soulvegr**

Your Level determines your **Epoch**—a tier of power that defines the scope of your abilities and the nature of stories you participate in. Advancement between Epochs is not automatic; reaching Level 11 or 21 unlocks the *potential* for the next Epoch, but you must fulfill specific narrative requirements to fully transition.

|Epoch|Levels|Status|AP per Level|Attribute Cap|Breakthrough Bonus|
|-|-|-|-|-|-|
|**Initiate**|**1–10**|**Mortal Hero**|**10 AP**|**20**|**—**|
|**Advanced**|**11–20**|**Living Legend**|**12 AP**|**25**|**+20 AP at Level 11**|
|**Ascended**|**21–30**|**Demigod**|**15 AP**|**30**|**+30 AP at Level 21**|

**Design Philosophy: Epochs describe narrative scale and story scope, not hard equipment restrictions. A wealthy or well-connected Initiate might acquire high-end Frames or premium SLDs early through credits, favors, or risky heists. Conversely, an Advanced character might still be trapped in a cheap Vessel due to circumstances. Gear availability comes from Credits, connections, cunning, and risk—not arbitrary level locks.**

1. #### **The Initiate Epoch (Levels 1–10): Mortal Heroes**

You are exceptional but mortal—talented, perhaps touched by fate, but still bound by the fragility of flesh and the limitations of your current circumstances.

**Typical Stories**:

* Planetary or regional threats
* Personal survival, debts, and vendettas
* First encounters with Divine Houses, soul-tech cults, or cosmic mysteries

**Common Resources**: Most Initiates use basic or scavenged SLDs (if their world even possesses such technology) and relatively fragile Vessels. **True Death** remains a very real threat.

2. #### **The Advanced Epoch (Levels 11–20): Living Legends**

You have transcended ordinary limits. Your name carries weight across star systems or entire kingdoms. Houses, factions, and cosmic forces begin to take notice.

**Requirements to Enter**: To fully transition into the Advanced Epoch at Level 11, you must fulfill **at least one** of the following:

1. **Mentor Bond**: Establish a meaningful relationship with a powerful figure—a living demigod, an Echo Ancestor, a soul-mentor bound to an artifact, or a divine emissary—who guides your growth.
2. **Heritage Awakening**: Experience a soul or bloodline awakening (see Section 5.4) that reveals deeper connections to cosmic forces, Realms, or ancient lineages.
3. **The Heroic Catalyst**: Perform a monumental act of heroism, survive a "limit break" moment, or make a sacrifice that permanently alters your Soul's resonance with reality.

**Typical Stories**:

* Regional wars, House intrigue, or factional conflicts
* Hunting artifacts or defending against cosmic threats
* Earning or stealing access to superior SLDs, Frames, and Divine Crystals

  3. #### **The Ascended Epoch (Levels 21–30): Demigods**

You are a nascent deity—a being who reshapes worlds, confronts pantheons directly, and walks among the divine as peer or challenger.

**Requirements to Enter**: To fully transition into the Ascended Epoch at Level 21, you must typically fulfill **all** of the following (or GM-approved equivalent paths):

1. **Mentor Legacy**: Complete your training or carry forward the legacy of a fallen mentor through a **Legacy Quest**—a final trial that honors, surpasses, or rejects their teachings.
2. **Pillar Trial**: Pass a trial that tests your mastery over one of the Three Pillars—**Vessel** (endurance, combat prowess), **Soul** (metaphysical will, Power mastery), or **Essence** (knowledge, identity, social influence).
3. **The Great Synthesis**: Achieve a defining moment of transformation—often involving sacrifice, revelation, or a choice that fundamentally changes your role in the cosmos and how reality responds to you.

**Typical Stories**:

* Multiversal conflicts and cosmic wars
* Direct confrontation with the 12 Houses or Eternal attention
* Reshaping civilizations, rewriting local reality, or claiming fragments of the Eternal Throne

**Practical Immortality**: At this tier, you likely possess the connections, resources, and reputation to secure the finest SLDs, backup Vessels, and reality anchors—making True Death rare but never impossible.

3. ### **5.3 Spending Advancement Points (AP)**

When you gain a Level, you receive AP to enhance your character. You may spend these points immediately or save them for more expensive upgrades.

1. #### **A. Attributes \& Skills**

|Category|Current Score/Rank|AP Cost to Increase (+1)|Requirement|
|-|-|-|-|
|**Attributes**|**10–15**|**2 AP**|**None**|
||**16–20**|**5 AP**|**None**|
||**21–25**|**10 AP**|**Advanced Epoch**|
||**26–30**|**15 AP**|**Ascended Epoch**|
|**Skills**|**Rank 0–5**|**2 AP**|**None**|
||**Rank 6–10**|**5 AP**|**None**|

2. #### **B. Powers, Perks \& Hindrances**

|Upgrade|AP Cost|Requirement|
|-|-|-|
|**Learn New Power (Tier 1)**|**5 AP**|**Relevant Skill Rank 1+**|
|**Upgrade Power to Tier 2**|**10 AP**|**Level 5+**|
|**Upgrade Power to Tier 3**|**15 AP**|**Level 11+ (Advanced Epoch)**|
|**Learn Divine Spark / Epic Ability**|**25 AP**|**Level 21+ (Ascended Epoch)**|
|**Standard Perk**|**10 AP**|**—**|
|**Remove Minor Hindrance**|**10 AP**|**Narrative justification required**|
|**Remove Moderate Hindrance**|**20 AP**|**Narrative justification required**|
|**Remove Major Hindrance**|**30 AP**|**Narrative justification required**|

**Removing Hindrances requires narrative justification—intensive training, therapy, surgical intervention, soul-cleansing rituals, or confronting the source of trauma.**

**Optional Rule**: GMs may allow players to spend AP toward special narrative goals such as establishing **Contacts**, **Safehouses**, **Faction Status**, or **Reputation** if it fits the campaign's tone.

4. ### **5.4 Soul Heritage Awakenings**

Some characters carry latent **soul-heritage**—fragments of ancient lineages, ties to specific Realms of Influence, or dormant connections to one of the 12 Divine Houses sleeping within their Soul.

A **Soul Awakening** is a sudden surge of cosmic potential triggered by intense stress, contact with powerful artifacts, or proximity to Realm-concentrated locations (Lattice Nodes, Breach Rifts, divine sanctums).

1. #### **Possible Triggers**
* Reaching Levels 5, 10, or 15
* Entering a Sanctum, Breach Rift, or Lattice Node
* Pushing a Power beyond its normal limits during a critical moment
* Near-death experience or True Death averted by extraordinary means

  #### **Possible Effects (GM-Mediated)**

* Gaining a new minor Power or Talent tied to a Realm (e.g., time-sense for Time, dream-walking for Dream, void-resistance for Void)
* Raising the cap for one Attribute by +2 beyond normal Epoch limits
* Altering "Soul Grade" narratively (e.g., Ember → Spark → Flame) to reflect increased metaphysical presence
* Unlocking a unique ability tied to bloodline or cosmic destiny

  **Awakenings are story events first, mechanical benefits second.** They should emerge organically from gameplay rather than being purchased or requested.

5. ### **5.5 Mentors and the Path of Guidance**

   Mentorship is a powerful narrative tool and an optional mechanical requirement for advancing into higher Epochs. A mentor shapes not just your abilities but your understanding of the cosmos and your place within it.

1. #### **Mentor Types**

   **Living Demigod**: A powerful entity who trains you directly. They may demand service, oaths, dangerous missions, or moral compromises in exchange for their knowledge.

   **Echo Ancestor**: A soul echo bound to your bloodline, SLD, or inherited artifact. They communicate through visions, dreams, or ghostly manifestations—carrying ancient memory, unresolved agendas, and wisdom from eras long past.

   **Soul-Avatar**: A fragment of a dead god or legendary hero bound to a relic, weapon, or sacred site. They grant forgotten techniques but may attempt to influence your choices or steer your destiny toward their own unfinished goals.

   **Dream Weaver**: A mentor who trains you purely through the mental or soul plane—ideal for long-distance guidance, secretive instruction, or when physical presence is impossible.

2. #### **Losing a Mentor**

   If your mentor dies, vanishes, betrays you, or is revealed as something other than what they seemed:

* **You retain all abilities already learned** from them
* **Further growth may require a Legacy Quest**—honoring their teachings, rejecting their path, reclaiming their stolen artifacts, or surpassing them in deed to prove your independence

  **Mentors are not mandatory** for all tables, but they provide an excellent narrative lever for GMs who want advancement to feel earned, personal, and deeply woven into the story rather than purely numerical.

4. # 

   1. ## **Section 6: Character Creation**

   In Soulvegr RPG, character creation is a flexible, point-based process that empowers you to build exactly the hero you envision. There are no rigid classes—only the **Six Core Attributes**, your **Vessel**, your **Soul**, and your **Essence** that define your legend. Whether you're a lab-born prototype fleeing corporate masters, a noble heir to a dying Divine House, or a spacer with a hidden cosmic destiny, your journey begins with a spark of potential and **150 Build Points** to shape it.

1. ### **6.1 Overview of Character Creation**

   Character creation in Soulvegr is classless and narrative-driven. You begin with **150 Build Points (BP)** to spend on your hero's fundamental nature. This system allows you to create a psychic starship pilot, a dwarven mech-engineer, or a divine paladin with equal ease.

1. #### **Key Principles**

   **Build Points (BP)**: Your currency for creation. Everything has a cost, but taking **Hindrances** grants additional BP to spend elsewhere.

   **Attribute Cap**: Core Attributes are capped at a score of **20** during character creation (the maximum for the Initiate Epoch).

   **The Three Pillars**: Every character is defined by:

* **Vessel**: Your physical body (flesh, synth-frame, war-chassis, hybrid)
* **Soul**: Your eternal spark residing in the astral plane
* **Essence**: Your mind, memories, personality, and identity

  **Soul Link Device (SLD)**: If your world possesses the technology, you begin with a basic SLD. Otherwise, your Vessel, Soul, and Essence remain fused through natural astral cords.

  **Frame / Vessel**: Your starting body. It can be upgraded, replaced, or destroyed throughout your journey.

  **Soul Grade**: All starting characters begin at **Grade 1: Ember**—a flicker of cosmic potential with the capacity to become a supernova.

2. ### **6.2 The Build Point (BP) System**

   You start with **150 BP**. Use the table below to allocate your points across the various facets of your character.

|Category|BP Cost|
|-|-|
|**Races**|**0 to 12 BP (based on Tier)**|
|**Backgrounds**|**5 BP (Basic) or 10 BP (Advanced)**|
|**Attributes**|**+1 Score = 2 BP (Scores 11–15); +1 Score = 5 BP (Scores 16–20)**|
|**Skills**|**2 BP per rank (Ranks 1–5); 5 BP per rank (Ranks 6–10)**|
|**Talents**|**30 BP each (innate, powerful gifts)**|
|**Perks**|**5 BP (Minor) or 10 BP (Standard)**|
|**Powers**|**5 BP (Tier 1); +10 BP (Tier 2 Upgrade)**|
|**Soul Link Device (SLD)**|**0 BP (Civic Flicker) to 20 BP (Certified Standard)**|
|**Frame / Vessel**|**0 BP (Scrap Flesh) to 15 BP (Combat Chassis)**|
|**Starting Gear**|**1,500 Credits (can purchase additional Credits with BP)**|

1. #### **Creation Limits**

**Skills**: Maximum rank of **10** at character creation.

**Powers**: Limited to **Tier 1** and **Tier 2** (Tier 3 requires Level 11+ and Advanced Epoch).

**Hindrances**: You may gain up to **+30 BP** by taking character flaws and complications.

3. ### **6.3 Step-by-Step Process**

Follow these nine steps to forge your hero.

1. #### **Step 1: Conceptualize Your Hero**

Decide who you are and where you fit in the infinite multiverse. Consider:

* A lab-grown prototype fleeing corporate creators in a neon Spire?
* Bastard heir of a minor Divine House raised on a medieval fantasy world?
* Search-and-rescue specialist on a fleet flagship, recovering stranded SLDs from derelict ships?
* Void-touched survivor who barely escaped an Aberrant incursion with fractured memories?

**Goal**: Define your role, motivation, and a compelling reason to adventure.

2. #### **Step 2: Choose a Race**

Select your ancestry from **Section 7: Races of the Cosmos**. Your race defines your Vessel's starting traits, physical capabilities, and cultural background.

|Tier|Cost|Example Races|
|-|-|-|
|**Tier 1 (Common)**|**0 BP**|**Human, Orc, Dwarf**|
|**Tier 2 (Uncommon)**|**5 BP**|**Elf, Goliath, Void-Touched**|
|**Tier 3 (Rare)**|**12 BP**|**Celestial, Aberrant Hybrid, Divine Scion**|

**Note: Tier 3 races often include a free Power Origin or innate Soul Awakening trait.**

3. #### **Step 3: Select a Background**

Choose a background from **Section 8: Backgrounds \& Occupations**. This represents your life and training before the campaign begins.

|Type|Cost|Benefits|
|-|-|-|
|**Basic**|**5 BP**|**Minor stat boosts, starting skill proficiencies, basic equipment**|
|**Advanced**|**10 BP**|**Significant stat boosts, free Power Origin, narrative hook or faction connection**|

4. #### **Step 4: Allocate Attributes (The Six Core Stats)**

All six Attributes begin at a baseline score of **10**. Use BP to raise them according to the following costs:

|Score Range|BP Cost per +1|
|-|-|
|**11–15**|**2 BP**|
|**16–20**|**5 BP**|

*Example: To raise Mind from 10 to 16 costs: (5 points × 2 BP = 10 BP) + (1 point × 5 BP = 5 BP) =* **15 BP total**\*.\*

5. #### **Step 5: Choose a Power Origin**

If you wish to use magic, advanced technology, psionics, or divine abilities, you must select a **Power Origin** (detailed in **Section 13: The Powers System**).

|Origin|Cost|Description|
|-|-|-|
|**Way of Steel (Martial)**|**Free**|**Mastery of weapons, tactics, and physical combat. Available to all characters.**|
|**Arcane / Divine / Technomancy**|**10 BP**|**Granted automatically by some Advanced Backgrounds or purchased separately.**|

6. #### **Step 6: Buy Skills \& Talents**

**Skills** (Section 9): Determine what your character can do.

**Cost**: 2 BP per rank (Ranks 1–5); 5 BP per rank (Ranks 6–10)

**Talents** (Section 10): Innate, game-changing gifts that set you apart.

**Cost**: 30 BP each

7. #### **Step 7: Choose Perks (Optional)**

Perks represent specialized training, biological advantages, or unique circumstances.

|Type|Cost|Example|
|-|-|-|
|**Minor**|**5 BP**|**"Tech-Savvy" (+2 to hacking checks)**|
|**Standard**|**10 BP**|**"Ambidextrous" (no penalty for dual-wielding weapons)**|

8. #### **Step 8: Take Hindrances (Optional)**

If you need more BP, accept **Hindrances** from **Section 11: Perks \& Hindrances**.

**Limit**: You may gain a maximum of **+30 BP** from Hindrances.

**Example Hindrances**:

* **Nightmares** (+5 BP): Recurring visions of the Void disrupt your rest.
* **Soul-Debt** (+10 BP): You owe a Divine House or criminal syndicate for your SLD.
* **Echo Scar** (+15 BP): A past Relink left permanent mental or physical trauma.

  9. #### **Step 9: Finalize Your Character**

Calculate your derived statistics and finish selecting gear.

**Health Points (HP)**: **10 + Vitality Score + Force Score** (modified by your Frame's durability rating)

**Energy Points (EP)**: **10 + Mind Score + Spirit Score** (modified by your SLD's capacity rating)

**Defense Value (DV)**: **10 + Force Modifier + Armor/Shield Bonuses**

**Starting Credits**: **1,500 Cr** (See Section 12 for Frames, SLDs, weapons, and equipment)

10. #### **Soul Link Device (SLD) Options**

|SLD Type|Cost|Survival DC|Relink Limit|Notes|
|-|-|-|-|-|
|**Civic Flicker**|**0 BP**|**15**|**3–5 uses**|**Standard civilian issue. Fragile but functional.**|
|**Black-Market Rig**|**5 BP**|**18**|**3–5 uses**|**+1 bonus to hacking. Unstable and illegal.**|
|**House-Indentured Core**|**15 BP**|**10**|**8–10 uses**|**Reliable, but the House owns your backup.**|

&#x20;   11. #### \*\*Frame / Vessel Options\*\*


|Frame/Vessel Type|Cost|Bonus|Drawback|Notes|
|-|-|-|-|-|
|**Scrap Flesh**|**0 BP**|**+1 Stealth**|**Fragile (-2 HP)**|**Disposable body. Low durability.**|
|**Labor-Synth**|**5 BP**|**+2 Force**|**-2 Stealth**|**Industrial work chassis. Obvious and bulky.**|
|**Combat Chassis**|**15 BP**|**+1 Vitality, Armor (DR 1)**|**Debt (500 Cr)**|**Military-grade. Expensive to maintain.**|

&#x20;       4. ### \*\*6.4 Soul Heritage: The Secret Seed\*\*


During creation, discuss with your GM whether your hero possesses an **Unknown Heritage**. You may not know your true lineage, why a mysterious mark appears on your soul, or which of the 12 Divine Houses claims you as kin.

Choosing **Unknown Heritage** costs no BP but serves as an open invitation for the GM to weave your character into the cosmic conflicts of the 12 Houses and the Great Game. As you advance, you may experience a **Soul Awakening** (see Section 5.4), revealing which Realm of Influence flows through your bloodline.

5. ### **Creation Example: The SAR Specialist**

**Jax**, a Human Search-and-Rescue (SAR) specialist for the Aegis Fleet.

**1. Concept**: A brave technician who thrives in the deadly environment of derelict starships.

**2. Race**: Human (0 BP)

**3. Background**: Fleet Academy – Basic (5 BP)

**4. Attributes**:

* Force 14 (8 BP)
* Mind 16 (15 BP)
* Vitality 12 (4 BP)
* Authority, Substance, Spirit 10 (0 BP)

**Total Attribute Cost**: 27 BP

**5. Power Origin**: Way of Steel (Free), Tech-Integration (10 BP)

**6. Skills**:

* Technology 5 (10 BP)
* Athletics 3 (6 BP)
* First Aid 4 (8 BP)

**Total Skill Cost**: 24 BP

**7. Talents**: Living Forge (30 BP) – allows rapid field repairs on ship hulls and equipment

**8. Perks**: Void-Adapted (5 BP) – bonuses in zero-gravity and vacuum environments

**9. Hindrances**: Nightmares (+5 BP) – haunted by memories of a failed rescue mission

**10. SLD**: Civic Flicker (0 BP)

**11. Frame**: Labor-Synth (5 BP)

**Final Totals**:

**BP Spent**: 101 BP

**BP Gained from Hindrances**: +5 BP

**Remaining BP**: 150 – 101 + 5 = **54 BP** (available for additional stats, Powers, or saved for future use)

**HP**: 32 (10 + 12 Vitality + 14 Force – 4 from Scrap Flesh = 32)

**EP**: 36 (10 + 16 Mind + 10 Spirit)

**DV**: 12 (10 + 2 Force modifier)

**Soul Grade**: Ember

5. # 
6. # 

   1. ## **Section 7: Races of the Cosmos**

Your lineage is the first mark of destiny etched upon your Soul. In Soulvegr RPG, race is more than appearance—it determines your starting resilience, natural aptitudes, and the fundamental nature of your **Vessel**, the physical body through which your Soul and Essence express themselves. Whether your people evolved on a low-tech frontier world, were engineered in a Spire biolab, or descend from something older than the gods themselves, your lineage shapes how you interact with the multiverse from your very first breath.

1. ### **7.1 Race Tiers \& The Baseline**

Races are divided into **tiers** based on their rarity, power level, and proximity to cosmic forces. All races have a base Speed of **30 feet** unless noted otherwise.

|Tier|BP Cost|Attribute Bonus|Notes|
|-|-|-|-|
|**Human**|**0 BP**|**+1 to any Attribute**|**Baseline versatility; found across all planes and tech levels**|
|**Tier 1**|**3 BP**|**+2 to Attributes**|**Common lineages; the backbone of galactic civilization**|
|**Tier 2**|**6 BP**|**+4 to Attributes**|**Uncommon or specialized; often evolved, hybrid, or engineered**|
|**Tier 3**|**12 BP**|**+6 to Attributes**|**Mythic God-Touched; echoes of the Eternal Design. Includes free Power Origin**|

**Attribute Points: Add these bonuses to your Attribute scores after allocating your base points.**  
*Example: If a Dwarf grants +2 Vitality and your base is 10, your new Vitality score is 12.*

**Small Races** (Halfling, Gnome, Goblin): Base Speed is **25 feet**, but they possess special traits that enhance evasion and maneuverability.

**Setting Note**: Not every race exists on every world. The infinite multiverse of the Soulvegr means some lineages are commonplace on one planet and mythical on another. A Synthoid is standard in a Spire megacity but would be a terrifying anomaly on a medieval frontier world. Work with your GM to determine which races fit your campaign setting.

2. ### **7.2 Humans (0 BP) – The Baseline**

The eternal survivors. Whether as high-tech spacers, feudal peasants, or neon-city operatives, humans are defined by their refusal to be categorized and their relentless drive to ascend beyond their limitations.

**Attribute Bonus**: +1 to any one Attribute of your choice

**Skill Proficiencies**: Choose any two skills

**Racial Traits**:

* **Adaptable**: Gain one free Minor Perk (5 BP value) at character creation
* **Elemental Hardening**: Choose Resistance to Fire, Cold, or Lightning damage
* **Speed**: 30 feet

  3. ### **7.3 Tier 1: Common Races (3 BP, +2 Attribute Points)**

|Race|Attribute Bonus|Skill Proficiencies|Key Traits|
|-|-|-|-|
|**Elf**|**+2 Mind**|**Arcana, Perception**|**Fey Ancestry: Advantage on saves vs. Charm effects<br />Poison Resilience: Advantage on saves vs. poison<br />Darkvision: 60 ft<br />Speed: 35 ft**|
|**Dwarf**|**+2 Vitality**|**Smithing, Survival**|**Dwarven Tenacity: Speed not reduced by difficult terrain or heavy armor<br />Relentless Stride: Dash as a Bonus Action<br />Speed: 25 ft**|
|**Halfling**|**+1 Authority, +1 Force**|**Diplomacy, Stealth**|**Lucky Footing: Reroll natural 1s on Force checks<br />Small Frame: Move through spaces of larger creatures<br />Speed: 25 ft**|
|**Orc**|**+2 Force**|**Athletics, Intimidation**|**Relentless: If dropped to 0 HP, drop to 1 HP instead (1/Long Rest)<br />Thick-Skinned: Resistance to non-magical bludgeoning damage<br />Speed: 35 ft**|
|**Goblin**|**+2 Mind**|**Stealth, Deception**|**Nimble Escape: Disengage or Hide as a Bonus Action<br />Silver Lies: Advantage on Deception to conceal identity<br />Speed: 25 ft**|
|**Troll-Kin**|**+2 Vitality**|**Athletics, Endurance**|**Regeneration: Heal 1 HP per turn if above 0 HP<br />Dense Musculature: Advantage on Force saves<br />Speed: 35 ft**|
|**Gnome**|**+1 Mind, +1 Substance**|**Technology, Investigation**|**Tinker's Intuition: Advantage on Technology checks to repair or disable devices<br />Small Frame: As Halfling<br />Speed: 25 ft**|
|**Spacer**|**+1 Mind, +1 Substance**|**Piloting, Technology**|**Zero-G Mastery: Speed 40 ft in zero gravity; Advantage on Athletics to maneuver<br />Vacuum Drilled: Hold breath for 10 minutes**|
|**Lizardfolk**|**+2 Vitality**|**Survival, Perception**|**Natural Armor: Base DV is 13 + Force modifier (unarmored)<br />Hold Breath: Hold breath for 15 minutes<br />Carnivore's Bite: Unarmed strike deals 1d6 + Force modifier piercing damage**|
|**Catfolk**|**+2 Force**|**Acrobatics, Stealth**|**Feline Agility: Double your speed until end of turn (recharge after moving 0 ft on a turn)<br />Claws: Unarmed strikes deal 1d4 + Force modifier slashing<br />Darkvision: 60 ft**|
|**Merfolk**|**+1 Vitality, +1 Spirit**|**Athletics, Survival**|**Amphibious: Breathe air and water<br />Aquatic Adaptation: Swim speed 40 ft<br />Water-Born: Advantage on Athletics checks while swimming**|
|**Construct-Born**|**+1 Substance, +1 Mind**|**Technology, Endurance**|**Tireless: Don't need sleep; meditate 4 hours for Long Rest benefits<br />Hybrid Anatomy: Advantage on saves vs. disease<br />Integrated Tool: One simple tool (lockpicks, repair kit) built into your body**|

|Race|Attribute Bonus|Skill Proficiencies|Key Traits|

|**Goliath**|**+4 Vitality**|**Athletics, Endurance**|**Powerful Build: Count as Large for carrying/shoving<br />Hardy: Add Vitality modifier twice when calculating HP per level<br />Speed: 35 ft**|

&#x20;    5. ### \*\*7.5 Tier 3: Rare \& Exotic Heritages (12 BP, \\+6 Attribute Points)\*\*


These twelve lineages are **God-Touched**—living echoes of the Eternal Design. Each carries a fragment of cosmic resonance in their very biology. Their Vessels are not merely bodies but conduits for forces most mortals will never comprehend.

**Special Benefit**: Choose one **free Power Origin** related to your racial heritage.

|**Echo Lord**|**+4 Spirit, +2 Mind**|**Time Channeling**|**Temporal Echo: Reroll any d20 (3/Long Rest)<br />Precognition: Cannot be Surprised; +5 to Initiative**|
|**Soul Weaver**|**+4 Spirit, +2 Vitality**|**Sanctuary Channeling**|**Soul Sight: See invisible creatures and soul grades<br />Empathic Transfer: Spend your HP to heal an ally within 30 ft**|
|**Void Eater**|**+4 Substance, +2 Vitality**|**Death Channeling**|**Null-Field: Regain EP whenever a spell targeting you fails<br />Magic Resistance: Spells have Disadvantage to hit you**|
|**True Titan**|**+6 Force**|**War Channeling**|**Unstoppable: Size Large; triple carrying capacity<br />Brute Force: Natural melee attacks deal 2d6 + Force modifier**|
|**Dreamborn**|**+6 Mind**|**Liberation Channeling**|**Dream Walk: Enter dreams of sleeping creatures within 100 ft<br />Illusory Form: Project false image of yourself (constant minor illusion)**|
|**Flameheart**|**+6 Substance**|**Dominion Channeling**|**Elemental Blood: Immune to fire damage; resistance to cold<br />Ignition: Melee attacks deal +1d6 fire damage**|

2. ## **Section 8: Backgrounds \& Occupations**

Your background is who you were before destiny called. It grants **Skill Proficiencies**, **Attribute Bonuses**, and a unique **Origin Trait**. Advanced Backgrounds grant **Power Origins**—your connection to the cosmic forces that fuel supernatural abilities across the multiverse.

1. ### **8.1 Defining Your Origin**

|Tier|BP Cost|Benefits|
|-|-|-|
|**Basic**|**5 BP**|**+1 to one Core Attribute, 2 Skill Proficiencies, 1 Origin Trait**|
|**Advanced**|**10 BP**|**+2 to one Core Attribute (or +1 to two), 2 Skill Proficiencies, 1 Unique Origin Trait, Free Power Origin**|

2. ### **8.2 Basic Backgrounds (5 BP)**

These foundations provide no Power Origins but represent common upbringings across the infinite multiverse.

|Background|Attribute Bonus|Skills|Origin Trait|
|-|-|-|-|
|**Rural Survivor**|**+1 Vitality**|**Nature, Animal Handling**|**Hardy Life: Advantage on saves vs. environmental hazards**|
|**Urban Orphan**|**+1 Force**|**Stealth, Streetwise**|**Street Tough: Reroll one failed Stealth or Deception check per session**|
|**Minor Nobility**|**+1 Authority**|**Diplomacy, History**|**Noble Bearing: Advantage on social checks with lower-status NPCs**|
|**Temple Acolyte**|**+1 Spirit**|**Religion, Insight**|**Sacred Shelter: Free lodging at temples of your faith**|
|**Academy Student**|**+1 Mind**|**Lore, Investigation**|**Well-Read: Once per session, ask GM for lore/history hint**|
|**Merchant's Apprentice**|**+1 Authority**|**Persuasion, Appraisal**|**Market Sense: Advantage on haggling and appraisal checks**|
|**Fleet Technician**|**+1 Mind**|**Technology, Piloting**|**Zero-G Training: No penalties in vacuum or zero-gravity**|
|**Lab Assistant**|**+1 Mind**|**Research, Medicine**|**Scientific Method: Advantage on Investigation for experiments**|
|**Frontier Scout**|**+1 Vitality**|**Survival, Perception**|**Pathfinder: Cannot become lost except by magical means**|
|**Factory Worker**|**+1 Substance**|**Crafting, Endurance**|**Industrial Training: Advantage on checks to operate heavy machinery**|
|**Entertainer**|**+1 Authority**|**Performance, Persuasion**|**Stage Presence: Advantage on Performance checks to gain attention**|
|**Dock Worker**|**+1 Force**|**Athletics, Intimidation**|**Hardened Hands: Advantage on checks to lift, carry, or break objects**|

3. ### **8.3 Advanced Backgrounds (10 BP)**

Each Advanced Background grants a **free Power Origin** aligned with one of the 12 cosmic forces.

|Background|Attribute Bonus|Skills|Origin Trait|Power Origin|
|-|-|-|-|-|
|**Eternal Vigil Initiate**|**+2 Spirit**|**Religion, Endurance**|**Timeless Awareness: Sense when beings attempt to alter time around you**|**Eternity Channeling**|
