24. ## **Section 15: The Universe**

The multiverse of Soulvegr is an **Omniverse** of colliding realities—a vast tapestry woven from myth and technology, magic and machine. Ancient soul-gods dwell in crystalline sanctuaries at the hearts of stars, scavenger fleets strip-mine dead worlds for Divine Crystals, and beneath it all, the 12 Realms of Influence turn the cosmic gears of existence.

This is a universe where souls are currency, where death is negotiable, and where reality itself can be bought, sold, or forged anew.

1. ### **15.1 The Known Multiverse**

The Soulvegr multiverse is not a single galaxy but an **interconnected web of planes, dimensions, and realities** linked by astral pathways, ley-line networks, and soul-roads. Civilizations range from medieval kingdoms unaware of the stars to post-singularity empires that harvest suns for power.

**The multiverse is organized into three broad categories:**

1. #### **The Spires (Core Civilizations)**

**Description**: The height of civilization—orbital megastructures surround paradise worlds, artificial intelligence manages infrastructure, and immortality through SLD technology is commonplace. Magic and technology are studied side-by-side in universities. Divine Houses maintain order through legal frameworks and overwhelming force.

**Technology Level**: Tier 3 (Teleportation, matter replication, soul-backup networks, planetary shields)

**Magic**: Institutionalized and regulated. Power Origins are licensed. Illegal channeling is punishable by soul-severance.

**Soul Economy**: Fully developed. Soul Shards are illegal but Credits are backed by certified soul-energy reserves. SLDs are mandatory for citizenship.

**Examples**:

* **The Celestial Throne** - Capital of House Dominion, an orbital palace the size of a moon
* **Veritium Prime** - Truth Realm headquarters, where every word spoken is recorded and truth is law
* **The Eternal Archive** - Repository of all knowledge, guarded by Eternity Channelers
* **Forge-World Primus** - Industrial heart of the Creation Realm, where planets are built to order

**Threats**: Bureaucratic tyranny, soul-debt slavery, corporate feudalism, sterile immortality

2. #### **The Frontier (Wild Zones)**

**Description**: The cosmic "Wild West"—frontier colonies on the edge of known space, independent stations in asteroid belts, pirate havens, and worlds where the Divine Houses' reach is weak. Here, scavengers hunt for Divine Crystals in ancient ruins, warlords battle over fuel and water, and survival depends on what you can scavenge, steal, or forge.

**Technology Level**: Tier 1-2 (Salvaged tech, jury-rigged ships, black-market SLDs)

**Magic**: Unregulated and wild. Hedge mages, rogue channelers, and self-taught mystics. Power Origins acquired through necessity, not training.

**Soul Economy**: Informal. Soul Brokers operate openly. SLDs are expensive luxuries—most people still rely on natural astral cords and accept True Death as inevitable.

**Examples**:

* **The Slag Belt** - Asteroid field filled with derelict ships and mining stations
* **Freeport Oblivion** - Lawless station where anything can be bought or sold
* **The Dust Barrens** - Desert worlds settled by refugees and outcasts
* **Wreckage Fields** - Graveyards of ancient wars, picked clean by scavengers

**Threats**: Pirates, warlords, resource scarcity, corporate raiders, soul-traffickers

3. #### **The Deep Void (The Uncharted)**

**Description**: The spaces between stars and planes—where reality thins, cosmic horrors dwell, and the laws of physics become suggestions. Here, the Tulip Freighters drift on impossible currents, Void blooms grow from cracks in space-time, and some ships that venture in never return... or return *wrong*.

**Technology Level**: Irrelevant. Technology malfunctions. Only soul-based Powers and reality-warping function reliably.

**Magic**: Raw and dangerous. The Void Realm bleeds through. Time flows strangely. The dead don't stay dead.

**Soul Economy**: Non-existent. Souls are exposed and vulnerable. SLDs fail. True Death is common.

**Examples**:

* **The Mourning Fields** - Where dead gods float as continent-sized corpses
* **Beacon Twelve** - A mysterious signal from deep space that drives listeners mad
* **The Bloom** - An expanding region where reality itself is infected by Void energy
* **The Ghost Roads** - Paths through space that exist in multiple timelines simultaneously

**Threats**: Void entities, reality collapse, temporal paradoxes, soul corruption, cosmic entities beyond comprehension

2. ### **15.2 The Twelve Divine Houses**

The "gods" of Soulvegr are not omnipotent deities but **Divine Houses**—ancient organizations of Ascended souls who embody the 12 Realms of Influence. They are bureaucracies, corporations, churches, and militaries rolled into one. Each House controls aspects of reality aligned with their Realm.

**The Houses rarely intervene directly in mortal affairs**, preferring to work through agents, champions, and complex political maneuvers.

|House|Realm|Symbol|Domain|Agents|
|-|-|-|-|-|
|**House Eternal**|Eternity|Infinity Spiral|Time preservation, oaths, endurance|Vigil Keepers, Oath Wardens|
|**House Veritas**|Truth|All-Seeing Eye|Law, investigation, revelation|Inquisitors, Truth-Seekers|
|**House Umbra**|Shadow|Crescent Moon|Espionage, secrets, hidden knowledge|Shadow Operatives, Spies|
|**House Artifex**|Creation|Hammer \& Anvil|Industry, construction, innovation|Architects, Engineers|
|**House Vitae**|Life|Tree of Life|Medicine, nature, growth|Healers, Druids|
|**House Chronos**|Time|Hourglass|Temporal research, prediction|Chrono-Mages, Oracles|
|**House Sanctuary**|Sanctuary|Shield|Protection, refugees, safe havens|Paladins, Guardians|
|**House Vortheon**|War|Crossed Swords|Military, strategy, conflict|Generals, War-Champions|
|**House Nullis**|Void|Empty Circle|Anti-magic, entropy, the spaces between|Void Walkers, Nullifiers|
|**House Mortis**|Death|Skull \& Rose|Afterlife management, necromancy regulation|Reapers, Psychopomps|
|**House Libertas**|Liberation|Broken Chain|Freedom, revolution, anti-tyranny|Rebels, Liberators|
|**House Dominion**|Dominion|Crown|Rule, control, hierarchy|Emperors, Subjugators|

1. #### **The Pantheons**

Divine Houses form **Pantheons**—alliances of 12 Houses (one from each Realm) that enforce cosmic law within their sphere of influence. Multiple Pantheons exist, often in competition or outright conflict.

**The Prime Pantheon** (most powerful, controls the Spires):

* Enforces soul-economy regulations
* Prosecutes illegal soul harvesting
* Maintains the Ley-Line network
* Prevents Void incursions
* Manages inter-planar treaties

**Rogue Pantheons** exist in the Frontier and beyond, with different priorities and methods.

3. ### **15.3 Planar Travel \& The Ley-Line Network**

The multiverse is connected by invisible pathways of soul-energy called **Ley Lines**. These cosmic highways allow FTL travel and instantaneous communication across vast distances.

1. #### **Ley-Drive Travel**

**How It Works**: Ships equipped with Ley-Drives "skip" along Ley Lines, traveling between star systems in days rather than centuries.

**Requirements**:

* **Pilot** (Force + Piloting): Controls the ship
* **Navigator** (Mind or Spirit + Mysticism): Plots the course through the astral plane
* **Functioning Ley-Drive**: Technology + soul-energy hybrid engine

**Dangers**:

* **Bad Navigation**: Getting lost in the Astral Plane, emerging light-years off course, or arriving decades late
* **Ley-Line Storms**: Turbulent soul-energy that can tear ships apart
* **Void Incursions**: Weak points where the Void Realm bleeds through
* **Pirates**: Ambush points along major trade routes

**Travel Times**:

* **Same system**: Hours to days (conventional propulsion)
* **Adjacent systems**: 1-3 days (Ley-Drive)
* **Cross-sector**: 1-2 weeks (Ley-Drive)
* **Cross-galaxy**: Months (requires multiple Ley-jumps and resupply)

  2. #### **Other Planes of Existence**

|Plane|Access Method|Primary Use|Dangers|
|-|-|-|-|
|**Astral Plane**|**Natural (all souls exist here)**|**Soul travel, Ley-Drive navigation**|**Astral predators, soul theft**|
|**Ethereal Plane**|**Phase Shift Powers, special tech**|**Stealth, bypassing barriers**|**Getting stuck, ghost possession**|
|**Elemental Chaos**|**Ritual gates, Creation Powers**|**Raw power harvesting, forging**|**Elemental storms, hostile entities**|
|**Shadow Realm**|**Shadow Channeling**|**Stealth travel, hiding**|**Madness, losing your way, shadow corruption**|
|**The Void**|**Tears in reality, Void Channeling**|**Unknown—most don't return**|**Everything**|
|**The Far Shore**|**Death**|**Where souls go after True Death**|**One-way trip (usually)**|

&#x20;    4. ### \*\*15.4 Cosmic Mysteries \& Legends\*\*

        1. #### \*\*The Eternal Throne\*\*


**The Legend**: At the center of the multiverse sits the Eternal Throne—a seat of absolute power that can rewrite reality itself. The 12 Divine Houses each seek one of the 12 Throne Fragments, ancient artifacts that supposedly grant access to the Throne's power.

**The Truth**: Unknown. Some scholars believe the Throne is metaphorical—a state of cosmic enlightenment. Others claim it's a literal chair in a hidden dimension. The Divine Houses definitely seek *something*, and they're willing to wage secret wars for it.

**Current Status**: 3 Fragments confirmed to exist, 9 unaccounted for. The hunt continues.

2. #### **The Godforge**

**The Legend**: A mythical device, location, or process capable of transforming mortals into gods—elevating Ascended souls to the level of Divine House founders.

**The Truth**: Something called the Godforge definitely existed. Ancient records reference it. Whether it still functions, where it is, and what it truly does remain unknown.

**Theories**:

* An ancient machine in the Deep Void
* A ritual requiring all 12 Throne Fragments
* A metaphor for the Ascension process itself
* The original name for the Eternal Throne

  3. #### **The Bloom (Entropy Wave)**

**The Phenomenon**: Stars on the galactic edge are going dark. Not exploding—simply ceasing to exist. The darkness spreads slowly but inexorably inward. Where stars vanish, Void blooms appear—zones of reality corruption.

**The Threat**: If unchecked, the Bloom will eventually consume the entire multiverse.

**Current Response**: The Divine Houses deny it's happening. Independent researchers who investigate tend to disappear. Frontier colonies in the affected zones are evacuated or abandoned.

**Theories**:

* Natural cosmic cycle
* Void Realm expansion
* Consequence of the Throne Fragment war
* Something awakening in the Deep Void

  4. #### **Tulip Freighters**

**Description**: Massive ships shaped like cosmic flowers, each the size of a moon, drifting through the Deep Void on unknown currents. Their hulls shimmer with impossible colors. They don't respond to hails.

**The Mystery**: What are they? Who built them? Where are they going? What's inside?

**Known Facts**:

* Boarding parties never return
* Ships that follow them too closely vanish
* They appear to be following specific routes
* Scans show impossible readings—spaces larger on the inside, temporal distortions, soul signatures that read as both living and dead

**Theories**:

* Ancient generation ships from before the Divine Houses
* Living entities disguised as vessels
* Prisons for cosmic threats
* Automated systems from a dead civilization

  5. #### **Soul Grades \& The Hidden Truth**

**Public Knowledge**: Souls are graded Ember (lowest) through Inferno (highest). Reaching Inferno allows Ascension to join the Divine Houses.

**Whispered Secret**: There are grades beyond Inferno. The Divine House founders possess souls that burn with colors that shouldn't exist. Seven cosmic grades beyond mortal comprehension.

**The Forbidden Knowledge**: Those who learn the true names of these grades tend to go mad, vanish, or be recruited into secret societies that operate outside normal reality.

**What Wise Ones Know**: The Seven Cosmic Grades are connected to fundamental forces that predate the Divine Houses. The Houses themselves may be afraid of what happens when mortals discover them.

6. #### **Beacon Twelve**

**The Signal**: A repeating transmission from deep space, beyond the edges of known reality. It pulses every 12 hours, broadcasting in a pattern that resembles language but isn't.

**The Effect**: Those who listen to the full signal report:

* Seeing colors that don't exist
* Understanding cosmic truths they can't articulate
* Feeling called to travel into the Deep Void
* Experiencing temporal displacement

**Official Response**: The signal is quarantined. Receiving equipment tuned to its frequency is illegal. The Divine Houses claim it's a natural phenomenon.

**Unofficial Response**: Secret expeditions have been launched. None have returned. House Chronos and House Truth are particularly interested but publicly deny involvement.

5. ### **15.5 Factions \& Organizations**

Beyond the Divine Houses, countless organizations operate across the multiverse:

1. #### **The Soul Brokers' Guild**

**Purpose**: Manage the legal and illegal soul economy  
**Alignment**: Neutral (profit-driven)  
**Power**: Immense economic influence  
**Operations**: Soul-debt collection, SLD manufacturing, resurrection services, memory trading

2. #### **The Liberation Front**

**Purpose**: End soul-slavery and tyrannical Divine House control  
**Alignment**: Chaotic Good (from their perspective)  
**Power**: Growing influence in the Frontier  
**Operations**: Freeing soul-debtors, sabotaging House operations, spreading revolution

3. #### **The Void Cult (The Bloom Shepherds)**

**Purpose**: Hasten the coming of the Bloom  
**Alignment**: Chaotic Evil (or Neutral—they claim it's inevitable)  
**Power**: Limited but growing  
**Operations**: Creating Void rifts, corrupting souls, hunting for "the ones who will survive"

4. #### **The Scavenger Guilds**

**Purpose**: Salvage, trade, and survival  
**Alignment**: True Neutral  
**Power**: Control critical resources  
**Operations**: Ruin diving, Divine Crystal hunting, ship salvage, black market tech

5. #### **The Order of the Eternal Vigil**

**Purpose**: Protect reality from cosmic threats  
**Alignment**: Lawful Neutral  
**Power**: Secret but significant  
**Operations**: Monitor the Bloom, guard against Void incursions, hunt rogue Ascended souls

6. ### **15.6 Daily Life in the Multiverse**

**In the Spires**:

* Most citizens have SLDs and don't fear death
* Work is often optional (automation handles most tasks)
* Social status is determined by soul grade and influence
* Politics are complex, layered, and often invisible to common people
* Main concerns: Boredom, soul-debt, loss of purpose

**On the Frontier**:

* Death is real and permanent for most
* Every day is a struggle for resources
* Freedom is valued above safety
* Community bonds are strong
* Main concerns: Survival, pirates, corporate exploitation

**In the Deep Void**:

* Few permanent settlements exist
* Reality is negotiable
* Every moment could be your last
* Those who thrive here are touched by something beyond mortal understanding
* Main concerns: Not going insane, not becoming something else, finding a way back

  25. ## **Section 16: Appendices**

This section provides essential tools for playing Soulvegr, including a comprehensive glossary, character sheet template, and quick-reference tables.

1. ### **16.1 Glossary of Terms**

   1. #### **General TTRPG Terms**
* **1d4, 1d6, 1d8, 1d10, 1d12, 1d20**: Polyhedral dice notation. "1d6" means "roll one six-sided die."
* **Action**: Primary activity on your turn (attack, use a Power, etc.).
* **Advantage**: Roll 2d20, take the higher result.
* **DC (Difficulty Class)**: Target number to meet or beat on a d20 check.
* **Disadvantage**: Roll 2d20, take the lower result.
* **GM (Game Master)**: The person who runs the game, controls NPCs, and narrates the world.
* **PC (Player Character)**: A character controlled by a player.
* **Round**: A 6-second cycle in combat where everyone takes a turn.
* **Turn**: Your portion of a round where you take your actions.

  2. #### **Soulvegr-Specific Terms**
* **Advancement Points (AP)**: Currency earned during play used to improve your character.
* **Astral Cord**: The natural connection between soul and body. Can be severed by death.
* **Build Points (BP)**: Currency spent during character creation to purchase abilities, skills, and traits.
* **Channeling Origin**: The source of your magical/supernatural powers, tied to one of the 12 Realms of Influence.
* **Core Attributes**: The six fundamental statistics (Authority, Force, Vitality, Mind, Substance, Spirit).
* **Defense Value (DV)**: Your resistance to being hit. Formula: 10 + Force Modifier + Armor Bonus (capped by armor's Max Force).
* **Divine Crystal**: Naturally occurring crystal embodying one of the 12 Realms of Influence. Used in high-tier crafting.
* **Divine Houses**: The twelve god-like organizations that rule the multiverse, each representing one Realm of Influence.
* **Energy Points (EP)**: Fuel for Powers. Formula: 10 + Mind Modifier + Spirit Modifier.
* **Epoch**: Power tier representing your advancement (Initiate Levels 1-10, Advanced 11-20, Ascended 21-30).
* **Frame**: A synthetic body your soul can inhabit (requires SLD technology).
* **Health Points (HP)**: Your physical durability. Formula: 10 + Vitality Modifier + Force Modifier.
* **Hindrance**: A character flaw that grants extra BP during creation.
* **Ley Lines**: Invisible pathways of soul-energy that allow FTL travel.
* **Ley-Drive**: Technology that allows ships to travel along Ley Lines.
* **Pantheon**: Alliance of 12 Divine Houses (one from each Realm) that enforces cosmic law.
* **Perk**: Minor passive advantage (costs 5-10 BP).
* **Power**: Any active ability that costs EP (magic, psionics, technomancy, martial techniques).
* **Realm of Influence**: One of the 12 fundamental cosmic forces (Eternity, Truth, Shadow, Creation, Life, Time, Sanctuary, War, Void, Death, Liberation, Dominion).
* **SLD (Soul Link Device)**: Technology that replaces natural astral cords, allowing body-swapping and soul backup.
* **Soul Anchor**: Artifact that prevents soul theft or separation.
* **Soul Artifact**: Item infused with soul energy or containing soul fragments.
* **Soul Broker**: Merchant who deals in soul-debt, SLDs, and resurrection services.
* **Soul Grade**: The quality/power of your soul (Ember → Spark → Flame → Blaze → Inferno → beyond).
* **Soul Shard**: Condensed soul energy. Highly illegal when harvested from unwilling victims.
* **Talent**: Major innate ability (costs 30 BP, limit 1 at creation).
* **True Death**: Permanent death when the soul is destroyed or cannot reconnect to a body.
* **The Bloom**: Expanding zone of reality corruption spreading from the galactic edge.
* **The Eternal Throne**: Legendary seat of cosmic power at the center of reality.
* **The Spires**: Core civilizations with advanced technology and Divine House control.
* **The Frontier**: Wild zones on the edge of civilization where law is weak.
* **The Deep Void**: Uncharted space where reality breaks down and cosmic horrors dwell.
* **Tulip Freighter**: Mysterious moon-sized ships drifting through the Deep Void. Origin and purpose unknown.
* **Vessel**: Your physical body, whether organic or synthetic.

3. ### **16.3 Quick Reference Tables**

   1. #### **Table 1: Core Mechanics**

|Mechanic|Formula|
|-|-|
|**Skill Check**|**d20 + Attribute Modifier + Skill Rank vs. DC**|
|**Attack Roll**|**d20 + Attribute Modifier + Combat Skill Rank vs. DV**|
|**Saving Throw**|**d20 + Attribute Modifier vs. Save DC**|
|**Hit Points (HP)**|**10 + Vitality Modifier + Force Modifier**|
|**Energy Points (EP)**|**10 + Mind Modifier + Spirit Modifier**|
|**Defense Value (DV)**|**10 + Force Modifier + Armor Bonus (capped by Max Force)**|
|**Initiative**|**d20 + Force Modifier**|
|**Save DC (for your Powers)**|**10 + Casting Attribute Modifier + Casting Skill Rank**|

&#x20;     2. #### \*\*Table 2: Difficulty Classes\*\*


|Difficulty|DC|Example|
|-|-|-|
|**Trivial**|**5**|**Climb a ladder with rungs**|
|**Easy**|**10**|**Calm a friendly animal**|
|**Moderate**|**15**|**Hack a basic security door**|
|**Hard**|**20**|**Disarm a trained warrior**|
|**Very Hard**|**25**|**Resist a divine command**|
|**Legendary**|**30**|**Navigate through a Void Bloom**|
|**Impossible**|**35+**|**GM's discretion for epic moments**|

&#x20;        3. #### \*\*Table 3: Power Costs\*\*


|Tier|BP Cost|Typical EP Cost|Availability|
|-|-|-|-|
|**Tier 1 (Base)**|**5 BP**|**2-4 EP**|**Level 1+ (Initiate Epoch)**|
|**Tier 2 (Improved)**|**+10 BP (15 total)**|**4-7 EP**|**Level 5+ (Initiate Epoch)**|
|**Tier 3 (Mastered)**|**+15 BP (30 total)**|**7-12 EP**|**Level 11+ (Advanced Epoch)**|

**Note: You must own Tier 1 before upgrading to Tier 2, and Tier 2 before upgrading to Tier 3.**

4. #### **Table 4: Attribute Improvement Costs**

|Score Range|BP Cost per +1|AP Cost per +1|
|-|-|-|
|**10 (Starting)**|**0 BP**|**—**|
|**11-15**|**2 BP**|**2 AP**|
|**16-20**|**5 BP**|**5 AP**|
|**21-25**|**10 BP**|**10 AP**|
|**26-30**|**15 BP**|**15 AP**|

**Maximum at Creation: 20**  
**Maximum Ever: 30**

5. #### **Table 5: Rest \& Recovery**

|Rest Type|Time Required|HP Recovered|EP Recovered|Special|
|-|-|-|-|-|
|**Short Rest**|**1 hour**|**Roll Hit Dice (up to half max)**|**Half maximum (round down)**|**Can be taken multiple times**|
|**Long Rest**|**8 hours**|**Full maximum**|**Full maximum**|**Regain all Hit Dice, remove 1 Exhaustion**|

6. #### **Table 6: Action Economy**

|Action Type|Limit per Turn|Examples|
|-|-|-|
|**Action**|**1**|**Attack, Cast a Power, Dash, Disengage, Dodge, Help, Hide, Search, Use Object**|
|**Bonus Action**|**1 (if available)**|**Some Powers, specific abilities**|
|**Reaction**|**1 per round**|**Opportunity Attack, some Powers**|
|**Movement**|**Up to your Speed**|**Can be split before/after Action**|
|**Free Actions**|**Unlimited (GM discretion)**|**Speaking, dropping an item, environmental interaction**|

&#x20;     7. #### \*\*Table 7: Condition Effects\*\*


|Condition|Effect|
|-|-|
|**Blinded**|**Can't see. Attacks against you have Advantage. Your attacks have Disadvantage.**|
|**Charmed**|**Can't attack the charmer. Charmer has Advantage on social checks against you.**|
|**Deafened**|**Can't hear. Automatically fail checks requiring hearing.**|
|**Frightened**|**Disadvantage on checks and attacks while source is in line of sight. Can't willingly move closer.**|
|**Grappled**|**Speed is 0. Can't benefit from Speed bonuses.**|
|**Incapacitated**|**Can't take Actions or Reactions.**|
|**Invisible**|**Impossible to see without special senses. Attacks against you have Disadvantage. Your attacks have Advantage.**|
|**Paralyzed**|**Incapacitated. Can't move or speak. Auto-fail Force and Vitality saves. Attacks against you have Advantage. Melee hits within 5 ft are automatic crits.**|
|**Petrified**|**Transformed to stone. Incapacitated. Can't move or speak. Unaware of surroundings. Resistance to all damage. Immune to poison/disease.**|
|**Poisoned**|**Disadvantage on attacks and checks.**|
|**Prone**|**Disadvantage on attacks. Attacks against you have Advantage if attacker within 5 ft, Disadvantage if farther. Costs half movement to stand.**|
|**Restrained**|**Speed is 0. Attacks against you have Advantage. Your attacks have Disadvantage. Disadvantage on Force saves.**|
|**Stunned**|**Incapacitated. Can't move. Can speak falteringly. Auto-fail Force and Vitality saves. Attacks against you have Advantage.**|
|**Unconscious**|**Incapacitated. Can't move or speak. Unaware. Drop everything held. Fall Prone. Auto-fail Force and Vitality saves. Attacks against you have Advantage. Melee hits within 5 ft are automatic crits.**|
|**Exhaustion**|**Cumulative levels (1-6). Each level worsens penalties. Level 6 = death.**|

&#x20;        8. #### \*\*Table 8: Exhaustion Levels\*\*


|Level|Effect|
|-|-|
|**1**|**Disadvantage on ability checks**|
|**2**|**Speed halved**|
|**3**|**Disadvantage on attack rolls and saving throws**|
|**4**|**Hit point maximum halved**|
|**5**|**Speed reduced to 0**|
|**6**|**Death**|

**Removal: One level removed per Long Rest (if adequate food/water).**

9. #### **Table 9: Cover**

|Cover Type|DV Bonus|Save Bonus|
|-|-|-|
|**Half Cover**|**+2**|**+2**|
|**Three-Quarters Cover**|**+5**|**+5**|
|**Full Cover**|**Cannot be targeted**|**Cannot be targeted**|

10. #### **Table 10: Travel Times (Ley-Drive)**

|Distance|Conventional Drive|Ley-Drive|
|-|-|-|
|**Same System**|**Days to months**|**Hours to days**|
|**Adjacent Systems**|**Years to decades**|**1-3 days**|
|**Same Sector**|**Centuries**|**1-2 weeks**|
|**Cross-Galaxy**|**Impossible**|**Months (multiple jumps)**|

&#x20;      4. ### \*\*16.4 GM Quick Reference\*\*

          1. #### \*\*Encounter Difficulty (per PC)\*\*


|Difficulty|Total Enemy CR|
|-|-|
|**Easy**|**CR = 1/4 × PC Level**|
|**Moderate**|**CR = 1/2 × PC Level**|
|**Hard**|**CR = PC Level**|
|**Deadly**|**CR = 2 × PC Level**|

**Example: For 4 Level 5 PCs, a Hard encounter = 4 enemies of CR 5, or 8 enemies of CR 2.5, etc.**

2. #### **Improvising Damage**

|Severity|Damage|
|-|-|
|**Minor hazard**|**1d6**|
|**Moderate hazard**|**2d6**|
|**Serious hazard**|**4d6**|
|**Deadly hazard**|**10d6**|
|**Instant death**|**20d6+**|

3. #### **Session Rewards**

|Achievement|AP Awarded|
|-|-|
|**Completing a session**|**3-5 AP**|
|**Major story milestone**|**+2 AP**|
|**Exceptional roleplay**|**+1 AP**|
|**Creative problem-solving**|**+1 AP**|
|**MVP of session**|**+1 AP**|

**Standard Range: 3-8 AP per session**
